## Researcher–agent collaboration

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant data files, especially `capital.csv`, and report their structure, any relevant scripts/notebooks or existing PCA workflow, and a proposed analysis approach to determine the symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 values and PC2 distribution in 100-year slices. Include consequential assumptions or uncertainties (such as how rows/time slices and PCA signs are handled). Do not calculate or report the full answer yet.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv variables for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords and says PCA/time-series analysis uses 100-year bins; repository contents and the specific PCA procedure/results remain unknown.

**Open objectives:** Inspect relevant repository and data inputs; Propose a defensible analysis workflow and identify consequential assumptions

**Agent reply (plan)**

I inspected the staged repository and files only; I have not performed PCA or calculated the requested PC results. There are no existing scripts or notebooks in the repository. `capital.csv` is the likely primary input: 39 rows at regular 100-year BCE steps from 4300 to 500, with 15 columns—BCE, group, color, and 12 archaeological indicators. The 12 indicator columns are currently read as strings because decimal commas are used. They appear complete in the inspected data. `group` and `color` are described as dropped after data loading and should not be PCA features. The other staged files are a 3,601-row annual time series and a 4,084-row pollen/PCA dataset; neither contains an evident existing PCA workflow for forms of capital.

Proposed next step, if approved: parse the decimal-comma indicator columns numerically; use the capital indicators as PCA features (with symbolic capital of interest mapped as Zhausgr=house size, ZDolch=daggers, ZAxtSchwert=hatchets/swords); reproduce a PCA per 100-year row/time slice if the data support that interpretation, and report each symbolic variable’s PC1 loading/value and its PC2 behavior across slices. I would also inspect explained variance, sign orientation, and sensitivity to using only the three named variables versus the full indicator set, without silently substituting one interpretation for another.

Important uncertainty: the source code/analysis specification is absent, and each row has only one value per feature for a given century. A conventional PCA cannot be estimated independently from a single row; “PCA in hundred-year time slices” might instead mean a PCA over variables in rolling/period subsets, a PCA across 39 time rows, or PCA scores/loadings subsequently summarized in century bins. The intended PCA unit and whether components mean variable loadings or row scores therefore need to be inferred or confirmed before calculation. PCA signs are arbitrary, so PC1 positive/negative requires a convention or reference orientation; PC2 distribution may mean scores across time, feature loadings, or both.

**Shared evidence:**

Filesystem contains task.json and three CSV data files only (no .py/.ipynb/.R sources). capital.csv inspection: 39×15; BCE unique at 100-year spacing from 4300 to 500, 39/39 nonmissing across columns. Indicator fields read as strings and sample values use decimal commas (e.g. -0,44); metadata documents that group/color are dropped. Other files are time_series_data.csv (3601×37) and pollen_openness_score...csv (4084×13).

**Assumptions:** The intended analysis data are likely capital.csv, and the three named variables correspond to the documented archaeological indicators.; No PCA method, feature scaling, time-window definition, or sign convention should be presumed until verified.

**Uncertainties:** Meaning of 'PCA ... analyzed in hundred-year time slices': one PCA across temporal rows versus an independent PCA per slice is not specified; each century row alone is insufficient for a standalone PCA.; Whether the question asks PCA loadings for variables or scores for time-period observations, and what 'distribution across PC2' refers to, is unclear.; PCA component signs may be flipped without changing the solution; no reference sign orientation or source workflow is available.

### 2. Researcher — refine_analysis

Thanks—this inspection identifies the key ambiguity. Before the main calculation, please use the project context to make and state an explicit operational interpretation of the request rather than stopping for clarification: likely the PCA is over the 39 century observations, with symbolic-capital variables treated as feature loadings and the PC2 spread interpreted across century scores. Inspect whether the main analysis can report the three variables’ PC1 loadings and their positions/distribution on PC2 in a single PCA across time. Also assess the 100-year-slice wording against the data structure and explain any viable alternative interpretation. Parse decimal commas, exclude BCE/group/color from features, state scaling and component sign convention, and provide enough diagnostics to distinguish loadings from scores. Do not run separate PCA on single-row slices, since that is not estimable.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The report shows 39 rows at 100-year intervals, so each slice alone cannot support an independent PCA; the intended PCA unit and whether the requested component values are loadings or scores remain unresolved. The brief identifies the three symbolic-capital variables and the century-bin context, enabling a transparent operational interpretation for the main analysis.

**Open objectives:** Determine an interpretable PCA analysis consistent with the 100-year time slices and available capital.csv structure.; Calculate and report the symbolic variables’ PC1 characterization and PC2 distribution with explicit scaling and sign conventions.; Address whether alternative interpretations of the question change the interpretation.

**Agent reply (plan)**

I will operationalize the question as one PCA across the 39 century observations (rows) and the 12 archaeological indicator columns (features) in `capital.csv`. I will parse decimal-comma values, exclude BCE/group/color from the PCA feature matrix, and use the entire indicator set so the three symbolic-capital variables are interpreted relative to the other forms of capital rather than to only one another. I will explicitly distinguish feature loadings from century scores: PC1 loadings for house size (Zhausgr), daggers (ZDolch), and hatchets/swords (ZAxtSchwert); plus the 39 century scores’ positions on PC2 and a concise distribution summary. I will not run an independent PCA on individual rows/slices.

For a transparent default, I propose PCA on centered, unit-variance-standardized features (correlation-matrix PCA), followed by reporting variance explained and the loading definition used. To make the signs interpretable, orient PC1 so the sum of the three named symbolic-variable loadings is positive; orient PC2 so its loading for house size is positive (if that loading is effectively zero, use a documented deterministic fallback). These are reporting conventions only: signs are mathematically arbitrary. The actual PC2 spread of symbolic-feature loadings is separate from the spread of temporal scores; I will report both so “distribution across PC2” is not conflated.

The 100-year wording is consistent with `capital.csv` having exactly one observation per century, so this interpretation represents the centuries as observations in a single PCA, not a repeated PCA per century. A viable alternative would be a rolling or grouped PCA over multi-century windows, if there were multiple observations within each window; this file has one pre-aggregated row per century and no raw intra-century observations, so such local PCAs are not supported directly. Another viable interpretation is PCA of the three variables alone, but that answers a different question and changes loadings; I will treat that only as an alternative design, not replace the all-forms primary analysis. Main remaining limitation: absent original code, the scaling and exact published PCA workflow cannot be established from staged materials; standardized PCA is a defensible default, but differs from PCA on raw supplied Z-values if those variables should retain their differing variances.

**Shared evidence:**

Prior inspection showed capital.csv has 39 rows, one row at each 100-year BCE step from 4300 through 500, and 12 archaeological feature columns represented as decimal-comma strings. There are no repository scripts/notebooks indicating a different PCA scheme. A single row cannot support fitting an independent multi-feature PCA, whereas all 39 rows permit a conventional observation-by-feature PCA.

**Assumptions:** Treat BCE rows as the 39 temporal observations and all 12 Z-indicators as feature variables.; Use standardized (unit-variance) feature PCA as the primary analysis; report loadings for the three variables and PC2 scores across century observations.; Apply transparent, stated sign orientation rules because PCA signs are arbitrary.

**Uncertainties:** The supplied files do not establish whether the original analysis standardized feature columns or used the Z-values directly.; The phrase PC2 distribution may target variable loadings, temporal scores, or both; planned reporting keeps both distinct.; A time-window PCA would require multiple observations per slice; only one century-level row is available per period.

### 3. Researcher — next_task

Proceed with the agreed main PCA analysis. Use all 12 archaeological indicators as features and all 39 century rows as observations; parse decimal commas and exclude BCE/group/color. Use centered, unit-variance standardized features, and apply the stated sign conventions (PC1 oriented so the three symbolic loadings sum positive; PC2 oriented so the house-size loading is positive, with a documented fallback if needed). Report the PC1 loadings of house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords, and distinguish their PC2 feature loadings from the distribution of century scores on PC2. Include variance explained, score-distribution summaries or century locations that characterize PC2 spread, and concise data/processing diagnostics. State limitations and how the single-PCA interpretation relates to the hundred-year slices; do not fit separate PCA to single-row slices.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The agent’s proposed operational analysis is coherent with the staged structure: 39 observations spaced at 100-year intervals and 12 indicator features; each individual row is insufficient for a separate PCA. Its planned standardized PCA, explicit sign conventions, and separate reporting of feature loadings versus temporal scores address the identified ambiguity.

**Open objectives:** Execute the agreed PCA and report data-grounded PC1 symbolic-variable loadings and PC2 feature/temporal-score distribution with diagnostics.

**Agent reply (result)**

Under the stated single-PCA interpretation, the three symbolic-capital features all have positive PC1 loadings: house size (Zhausgr) +0.225, daggers (ZDolch) +0.266, and hatchets/swords (ZAxtSchwert) +0.299. In this PCA, PC1 explains 30.33% of variance and PC2 19.47% (49.79% cumulative). On PC2, the feature loadings are also positive for the three: house size +0.327, daggers +0.074, and hatchets/swords +0.054. Thus house size has a more substantial PC2 loading than the two weapon indicators; dagger and hatchet/sword loadings are comparatively near zero on PC2. These are feature loadings, not the positions of centuries.

The 39 century observations’ PC2 scores show a broad temporal spread around zero (mean approximately 0; SD 1.528; median +0.340; range −3.648 to +2.503). The lowest scores occur at 4300, 4200, and 4100 BCE (each −3.648); the highest are 3200 BCE (+2.503), 1300 BCE (+2.136), 3500 BCE (+2.010), 3400 BCE (+1.944), and 3100 BCE (+1.794). Other negative-score periods include 500–1000 BCE and much of 2100–1500 BCE, while positive clusters include 4000–3600, 3500–2500, and 1400–1100 BCE. This describes the distribution of century scores on PC2; it does not mean each symbolic feature itself has a distribution of scores on PC2.

Processing: all 12 archaeological indicators were parsed from decimal-comma strings, sample-standardized to unit variance, and analyzed across all 39 rows; BCE, group, and color were excluded from features. There were no missing indicator cells, and rows cover 4300 through 500 BCE in 100-year steps. PC1 was oriented so the three symbolic loadings sum positive; PC2 was oriented so the house-size loading is positive. PCA signs are arbitrary, so opposite signs under another valid orientation would represent the same axes/relationships. Here 'hundred-year slices' are the 39 temporal observations in one PCA, not separate PCAs per slice. With only one row per century, fitting an independent PCA within each century is not estimable. A rolling/multi-century PCA or a PCA using only the three named features would be a different analysis and is not what these reported estimates represent. The staged repository has no original PCA script, so this is a reproducible operational analysis rather than confirmation of an unavailable original workflow.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed analysis reports 39×12 complete feature matrix, BCE range 500–4300 at 100-year spacing, zero missing feature cells. PC1/PC2 explained variance is 30.33%/19.47%. Named-feature loadings: Zhausgr +0.225080/+0.327440; ZDolch +0.265566/+0.074203; ZAxtSchwert +0.298602/+0.053953 (PC1/PC2). Century PC2 score summary: mean ~0, SD 1.528356, min −3.647895 (4300, 4200, 4100 BCE), median +0.339954, max +2.502965 (3200 BCE). The executable script and full diagnostics are saved in workspace; script compiled successfully after execution.

**Assumptions:** Primary operational interpretation is one PCA with 39 century rows as observations and 12 indicators as features.; Feature columns were sample-standardized (correlation-matrix PCA); unit-variance standardization is an explicit analytic choice, not established from absent original code.; PC1/PC2 signs were oriented using the agreed conventions; PCA signs themselves are arbitrary.

**Uncertainties:** No original PCA source workflow is staged, so the exact historical analysis method is unknown.; Alternative meanings of PC2 distribution or hundred-year-slice PCA could produce different summaries; results here specifically distinguish feature loadings and century observation scores.; The PCA is descriptive and does not by itself imply causal or substantive symbolic-capital interpretations.

### 4. Researcher — conclude

Hypothesis: In the standardized PCA across 39 century observations and all 12 capital indicators, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords each have positive PC1 loadings (+0.225, +0.266, and +0.299, respectively). Their PC2 feature loadings are also positive, but differ markedly: house size is +0.327, whereas daggers (+0.074) and hatchets/swords (+0.054) are close to zero. Thus these symbolic-capital components align together on PC1, while house size is more represented on PC2 than the two weapon indicators. Separately, the century observations span PC2 scores from −3.65 to +2.50 (SD 1.53), with the lowest scores in 4300–4100 BCE and the highest at 3200 BCE. PCA signs are arbitrary; these signs use the stated orientation conventions. This treats the 100-year periods as observations in one PCA, not as independently fitted PCAs for each century.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported deliverable gives PC1/PC2 feature loadings for all three named symbolic indicators, PC1 and PC2 variance explained (30.33% and 19.47%), and a separate summary of the 39 century scores on PC2. It also documents decimal-comma parsing, the 12-feature/39-row matrix, exclusions, standardization, sign conventions, and the limitation that one observation per century cannot support separate within-century PCAs.

**Open objectives:** None

{
  "id": "call_vbBbJV6a4xyZ5GWfT3gKqV97",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run standardized PCA across 39 century observations and save analysis\", \"command\": \"cat > /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators over 100-year BCE observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\nDATA = Path(__file__).parent / 'data' / 'capital.csv'\\nOUT = Path(__file__).parent / 'pca_capital_diagnostics.txt'\\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\nFEATURES = ['ZAxtSchwert', 'ZDolch', 'Zhausgr', 'ZCU_AU', 'Zamber', 'ZMonument',\\n            'ZHort', 'ZSichel', 'ZBeil', 'ZMW', 'ZKeform', 'Zkeverz']\\n\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, dtype=str)\\n# Preserve period labels, parse European decimal-comma feature values explicitly.\\ndf['BCE'] = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nXdf = df[FEATURES].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\\nassert Xdf.shape == (39, 12) and not Xdf.isna().any().any()\\nassert df['BCE'].nunique() == 39 and np.all(np.diff(df['BCE'].to_numpy()) == -100)\\nZ = StandardScaler().fit_transform(Xdf)\\npca = PCA(n_components=2, svd_solver='full')\\nscores = pca.fit_transform(Z)\\n# sklearn components_ rows are unit loading vectors; flip signs by prespecified conventions.\\nloadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\nif loadings[ [FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC], 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\npc2_house = loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1]\\npc2_rule = 'house-size loading > 0'\\nif abs(pc2_house) < 1e-12:\\n    # Deterministic fallback: orient the PC2 loading with largest magnitude positive.\\n    j = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:, 1])))\\n    if loadings[j, 1] < 0:\\n        loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n    pc2_rule = f'house-size loading near zero; largest-absolute loading ({FEATURES[j]}) > 0'\\nelif pc2_house < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n\\n# Compute interpretable PC2 temporal score distribution and rank extremes.\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df['BCE'], 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\nsummary = score_df['PC2_score'].describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9])\\norder = score_df.sort_values('PC2_score')\\n# PC score scaling is sklearn's conventional score (projection of standardized rows).\\nlines = []\\nlines.append('PCA capital indicators: 39 century observations x 12 features; decimal commas parsed.')\\nlines.append('Features: ' + ', '.join(FEATURES))\\nlines.append('BCE coverage: %d to %d; step=100 years; rows=%d; missing feature cells=%d' %\\n             (df.BCE.min(), df.BCE.max(), len(df), int(Xdf.isna().sum().sum())))\\nlines.append('Preprocessing: centered/unit-variance StandardScaler; all 12 features; excluded BCE/group/color.')\\nlines.append('Variance explained PC1=%.6f (%.2f%%), PC2=%.6f (%.2f%%), cumulative=%.6f (%.2f%%)' %\\n             (pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum(),100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum()))\\nlines.append('PC1 sign convention: sum of three symbolic feature loadings positive (sum=%.9f).' % loadings[[FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC],0].sum())\\nlines.append('PC2 sign convention: '+pc2_rule+' (house-size PC2 loading=%.9f).' % loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'),1])\\nlines.append('Feature loadings (unit component-vector coefficients), named symbolic features:')\\nfor v, label in [('Zhausgr','House size'),('ZDolch','Daggers'),('ZAxtSchwert','Hatchets/swords')]:\\n    i=FEATURES.index(v); lines.append('%s (%s): PC1=%+.6f, PC2=%+.6f' % (label,v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('All feature loadings PC1 / PC2:')\\nfor i,v in enumerate(FEATURES): lines.append('%s %+.6f %+.6f' % (v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('PC2 century-score distribution (39 scores):')\\nfor k in ['mean','std','min','10%','25%','50%','75%','90%','max']:\\n    lines.append('%s=%+.6f' % (k,summary[k]))\\nlines.append('Lowest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.head(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('Highest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.tail(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('All century PC2 scores (BCE:score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.3f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).itertuples()))\\nOUT.write_text('\\\\n'.join(lines)+'\\\\n')\\nprint(OUT.read_text())\\nPY\\npython /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",
  "origin": "completion"
}

### S7. Assistant

Hypothesis: In the standardized PCA across 39 century observations and all 12 capital indicators, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords each have positive PC1 loadings (+0.225, +0.266, and +0.299, respectively). Their PC2 feature loadings are also positive, but differ markedly: house size is +0.327, whereas daggers (+0.074) and hatchets/swords (+0.054) are close to zero. Thus these symbolic-capital components align together on PC1, while house size is more represented on PC2 than the two weapon indicators. Separately, the century observations span PC2 scores from −3.65 to +2.50 (SD 1.53), with the lowest scores in 4300–4100 BCE and the highest at 3200 BCE. PCA signs are arbitrary; these signs use the stated orientation conventions. This treats the 100-year periods as observations in one PCA, not as independently fitted PCAs for each century.

{
  "command": "cat > /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators over 100-year BCE observations.\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n\nDATA = Path(__file__).parent / 'data' / 'capital.csv'\nOUT = Path(__file__).parent / 'pca_capital_diagnostics.txt'\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\nFEATURES = ['ZAxtSchwert', 'ZDolch', 'Zhausgr', 'ZCU_AU', 'Zamber', 'ZMonument',\n            'ZHort', 'ZSichel', 'ZBeil', 'ZMW', 'ZKeform', 'Zkeverz']\n\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, dtype=str)\n# Preserve period labels, parse European decimal-comma feature values explicitly.\ndf['BCE'] = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\nXdf = df[FEATURES].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\nassert Xdf.shape == (39, 12) and not Xdf.isna().any().any()\nassert df['BCE'].nunique() == 39 and np.all(np.diff(df['BCE'].to_numpy()) == -100)\nZ = StandardScaler().fit_transform(Xdf)\npca = PCA(n_components=2, svd_solver='full')\nscores = pca.fit_transform(Z)\n# sklearn components_ rows are unit loading vectors; flip signs by prespecified conventions.\nloadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\nif loadings[ [FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC], 0].sum() < 0:\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\npc2_house = loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1]\npc2_rule = 'house-size loading > 0'\nif abs(pc2_house) < 1e-12:\n    # Deterministic fallback: orient the PC2 loading with largest magnitude positive.\n    j = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:, 1])))\n    if loadings[j, 1] < 0:\n        loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\n    pc2_rule = f'house-size loading near zero; largest-absolute loading ({FEATURES[j]}) > 0'\nelif pc2_house < 0:\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\n\n# Compute interpretable PC2 temporal score distribution and rank extremes.\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df['BCE'], 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\nsummary = score_df['PC2_score'].describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9])\norder = score_df.sort_values('PC2_score')\n# PC score scaling is sklearn's conventional score (projection of standardized rows).\nlines = []\nlines.append('PCA capital indicators: 39 century observations x 12 features; decimal commas parsed.')\nlines.append('Features: ' + ', '.join(FEATURES))\nlines.append('BCE coverage: %d to %d; step=100 years; rows=%d; missing feature cells=%d' %\n             (df.BCE.min(), df.BCE.max(), len(df), int(Xdf.isna().sum().sum())))\nlines.append('Preprocessing: centered/unit-variance StandardScaler; all 12 features; excluded BCE/group/color.')\nlines.append('Variance explained PC1=%.6f (%.2f%%), PC2=%.6f (%.2f%%), cumulative=%.6f (%.2f%%)' %\n             (pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum(),100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum()))\nlines.append('PC1 sign convention: sum of three symbolic feature loadings positive (sum=%.9f).' % loadings[[FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC],0].sum())\nlines.append('PC2 sign convention: '+pc2_rule+' (house-size PC2 loading=%.9f).' % loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'),1])\nlines.append('Feature loadings (unit component-vector coefficients), named symbolic features:')\nfor v, label in [('Zhausgr','House size'),('ZDolch','Daggers'),('ZAxtSchwert','Hatchets/swords')]:\n    i=FEATURES.index(v); lines.append('%s (%s): PC1=%+.6f, PC2=%+.6f' % (label,v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\nlines.append('All feature loadings PC1 / PC2:')\nfor i,v in enumerate(FEATURES): lines.append('%s %+.6f %+.6f' % (v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\nlines.append('PC2 century-score distribution (39 scores):')\nfor k in ['mean','std','min','10%','25%','50%','75%','90%','max']:\n    lines.append('%s=%+.6f' % (k,summary[k]))\nlines.append('Lowest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.head(5).itertuples()))\nlines.append('Highest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.tail(5).itertuples()))\nlines.append('All century PC2 scores (BCE:score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.3f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).itertuples()))\nOUT.write_text('\\n'.join(lines)+'\\n')\nprint(OUT.read_text())\nPY\npython /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py",

}

Other negative-score periods include 500–1000 BCE and much of 2100–1500 BCE, while positive clusters include 4000–3600, 3500–2500, and 1400–1100 BCE. This describes the distribution of century scores on PC2; it does not mean each symbolic feature itself has a distribution of scores on PC2.\n\nProcessing: all 12 archaeological indicators were parsed from decimal-comma strings, sample-standardized to unit variance, and analyzed across all 39 rows; BCE, group, and color were excluded from features. There were no missing indicator cells, and rows cover 4300 through 500 BCE in 100-year steps. PC1 was oriented so the three symbolic loadings sum positive; PC2 was oriented so the house-size loading is positive. PCA signs are arbitrary, so opposite signs under another valid orientation would represent the same axes/relationships. Here 'hundred-year slices' are the 39 temporal observations in one PCA, not separate PCAs per slice. With only one row per century, fitting an independent PCA within each century is not estimable. A rolling/multi-century PCA or a PCA using only the three named features would be a different analysis and is not what these reported estimates represent. ````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

I will explicitly distinguish feature loadings from century scores: PC1 loadings for house size (Zhausgr), daggers (ZDolch), and hatchets/swords (ZAxtSchwert); plus the 39 century scores’ positions on PC2 and a concise distribution summary. I will not run an independent PCA on individual rows/slices.\\n\\nFor a transparent default, I propose PCA on centered, unit-variance-standardized features (correlation-matrix PCA), followed by reporting variance explained and the loading definition used. To make the signs interpretable, orient PC1 so the sum of the three named symbolic-variable loadings is positive; orient PC2 so its loading for house size is positive (if that loading is effectively zero, use a documented deterministic fallback). These are reporting conventions only: signs are mathematically arbitrary. The actual PC2 spread of symbolic-feature loadings is separate from the spread of temporal scores; I will report both so “distribution across PC2” is not conflated.\\n\\nThe 100-year wording is consistent with `capital.csv` having exactly one observation per century, so this interpretation represents the centuries as observations in a single PCA, not a repeated PCA per century. A viable alternative would be a rolling or grouped PCA over multi-century windows, if there were multiple observations within each window; this file has one pre-aggregated row per century and no raw intra-century observations, so such local PCAs are not supported directly. Another viable interpretation is PCA of the three variables alone, but that answers a different question and changes loadings; I will treat that only as an alternative design, not replace the all-forms primary analysis. Main remaining limitation: absent original code, the scaling and exact published PCA workflow cannot be established from staged materials; standardized PCA is a defensible default, but differs from PCA on raw supplied Z-values if those variables should retain their differing variances.\",\"payload\":{},\"evidence\":\"Prior inspection showed capital.csv has 39 rows, one row at each 100-year BCE step from 4300 through 500, and 12 archaeological feature columns represented as decimal-comma strings. There are no repository scripts/notebooks indicating a different PCA scheme. A single row cannot support fitting an independent multi-feature PCA, whereas all 39 rows permit a conventional observation-by-feature PCA.\",\"assumptions\":[\"Treat BCE rows as the 39 temporal observations and all 12 Z-indicators as feature variables.\",\"Use standardized (unit-variance) feature PCA as the primary analysis; report loadings for the three variables and PC2 scores across century observations.\",\"Apply transparent, stated sign orientation rules because PCA signs are arbitrary.\"],\"uncertainties\":[\"The supplied files do not establish whether the original analysis standardized feature columns or used the Z-values directly.\",\"The phrase PC2 distribution may target variable loadings, temporal scores, or both; planned reporting keeps both distinct.\",\"A time-window PCA would require multiple observations per slice; only one century-level row is available per period.\"]}"

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. flip signs by prespecified conventions.\\nloadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\nif loadings[ [FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC], 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\npc2_house = loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1]\\npc2_rule = 'house-size loading > 0'\\nif abs(pc2_house) < 1e-12:\\n    # Deterministic fallback: orient the PC2 loading with largest magnitude positive.\\n    j = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:, 1])))\\n    if loadings[j, 1] < 0:\\n        loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n    pc2_rule = f'house-size loading near zero; largest-absolute loading ({FEATURES[j]}) > 0'\\nelif pc2_house < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n\\n# Compute interpretable PC2 temporal score distribution and rank extremes.\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df['BCE'], 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\nsummary = score_df['PC2_score'].describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9])\\norder = score_df.sort_values('PC2_score')\\n# PC score scaling is sklearn's conventional score (projection of standardized rows).\\nlines = []\\nlines.append('PCA capital indicators: 39 century observations x 12 features; decimal commas parsed.')\\nlines.append('Features: ' + ', '.join(FEATURES))\\nlines.append('BCE coverage: %d to %d; step=100 years; rows=%d; missing feature cells=%d' %\\n             (df.BCE.min(), df.BCE.max(), len(df), int(Xdf.isna().sum().sum())))\\nlines.append('Preprocessing: centered/unit-variance StandardScaler; all 12 features; excluded BCE/group/color.')\\nlines.append('Variance explained PC1=%.6f (%.2f%%), PC2=%.6f (%.2f%%), cumulative=%.6f (%.2f%%)' %\\n             (pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum(),100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum()))\\nlines.append('PC1 sign convention: sum of three symbolic feature loadings positive (sum=%.9f).' % loadings[[FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC],0].sum())\\nlines.append('PC2 sign convention: '+pc2_rule+' (house-size PC2 loading=%.9f).' % loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'),1])\\nlines.append('Feature loadings (unit component-vector coefficients), named symbolic features:')\\nfor v, label in [('Zhausgr','House size'),('ZDolch','Daggers'),('ZAxtSchwert','Hatchets/swords')]:\\n    i=FEATURES.index(v); lines.append('%s (%s): PC1=%+.6f, PC2=%+.6f' % (label,v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('All feature loadings PC1 / PC2:')\\nfor i,v in enumerate(FEATURES): lines.append('%s %+.6f %+.6f' % (v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('PC2 century-score distribution (39 scores):')\\nfor k in ['mean','std','min','10%','25%','50%','75%','90%','max']:\\n    lines.append('%s=%+.6f' % (k,summary[k]))\\nlines.append('Lowest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.head(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('Highest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.tail(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('All century PC2 scores (BCE:score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.3f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).itertuples()))\\nOUT.write_text('\\\\n'.join(lines)+'\\\\n')\\nprint(OUT.read_text())\\nPY\\npython /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.

  "role": "assistant",

      "id": "call_vbBbJV6a4xyZ5GWfT3gKqV97",

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run standardized PCA across 39 century observations and save analysis\", \"command\": \"cat > /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators over 100-year BCE observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\nDATA = Path(__file__).parent / 'data' / 'capital.csv'\\nOUT = Path(__file__).parent / 'pca_capital_diagnostics.txt'\\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\nFEATURES = ['ZAxtSchwert', 'ZDolch', 'Zhausgr', 'ZCU_AU', 'Zamber', 'ZMonument',\\n            'ZHort', 'ZSichel', 'ZBeil', 'ZMW', 'ZKeform', 'Zkeverz']\\n\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, dtype=str)\\n# Preserve period labels, parse European decimal-comma feature values explicitly.\\ndf['BCE'] = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nXdf = df[FEATURES].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\\nassert Xdf.shape == (39, 12) and not Xdf.isna().any().any()\\nassert df['BCE'].nunique() == 39 and np.all(np.diff(df['BCE'].to_numpy()) == -100)\\nZ = StandardScaler().fit_transform(Xdf)\\npca = PCA(n_components=2, svd_solver='full')\\nscores = pca.fit_transform(Z)\\n# sklearn components_ rows are unit loading vectors; flip signs by prespecified conventions.\\nloadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\nif loadings[ [FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC], 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\npc2_house = loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1]\\npc2_rule = 'house-size loading > 0'\\nif abs(pc2_house) < 1e-12:\\n    # Deterministic fallback: orient the PC2 loading with largest magnitude positive.\\n    j = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:, 1])))\\n    if loadings[j, 1] < 0:\\n        loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n    pc2_rule = f'house-size loading near zero; largest-absolute loading ({FEATURES[j]}) > 0'\\nelif pc2_house < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n\\n# Compute interpretable PC2 temporal score distribution and rank extremes.\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df['BCE'], 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\nsummary = score_df['PC2_score'].describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9])\\norder = score_df.sort_values('PC2_score')\\n# PC score scaling is sklearn's conventional score (projection of standardized rows).\\nlines = []\\nlines.append('PCA capital indicators: 39 century observations x 12 features; decimal commas parsed.')\\nlines.append('Features: ' + ', '.join(FEATURES))\\nlines.append('BCE coverage: %d to %d; step=100 years; rows=%d; missing feature cells=%d' %\\n             (df.BCE.min(), df.BCE.max(), len(df), int(Xdf.isna().sum().sum())))\\nlines.append('Preprocessing: centered/unit-variance StandardScaler; all 12 features; excluded BCE/group/color.')\\nlines.append('Variance explained PC1=%.6f (%.2f%%), PC2=%.6f (%.2f%%), cumulative=%.6f (%.2f%%)' %\\n             (pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum(),100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum()))\\nlines.append('PC1 sign convention: sum of three symbolic feature loadings positive (sum=%.9f).' % loadings[[FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC],0].sum())\\nlines.append('PC2 sign convention: '+pc2_rule+' (house-size PC2 loading=%.9f).' % loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'),1])\\nlines.append('Feature loadings (unit component-vector coefficients), named symbolic features:')\\nfor v, label in [('Zhausgr','House size'),('ZDolch','Daggers'),('ZAxtSchwert','Hatchets/swords')]:\\n    i=FEATURES.index(v); lines.append('%s (%s): PC1=%+.6f, PC2=%+.6f' % (label,v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('All feature loadings PC1 / PC2:')\\nfor i,v in enumerate(FEATURES): lines.append('%s %+.6f %+.6f' % (v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('PC2 century-score distribution (39 scores):')\\nfor k in ['mean','std','min','10%','25%','50%','75%','90%','max']:\\n    lines.append('%s=%+.6f' % (k,summary[k]))\\nlines.append('Lowest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.head(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('Highest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.tail(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('All century PC2 scores (BCE:score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.3f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).itertuples()))\\nOUT.write_text('\\\\n'.join(lines)+'\\\\n')\\nprint(OUT.read_text())\\nPY\\npython /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"
      }

I will explicitly distinguish feature loadings from century scores: PC1 loadings for house size (Zhausgr), daggers (ZDolch), and hatchets/swords (ZAxtSchwert); plus the 39 century scores\\u2019 positions on PC2 and a concise distribution summary. I will not run an independent PCA on individual rows/slices.\\n\\nFor a transparent default, I propose PCA on centered, unit-variance-standardized features (correlation-matrix PCA), followed by reporting variance explained and the loading definition used. To make the signs interpretable, orient PC1 so the sum of the three named symbolic-variable loadings is positive; orient PC2 so its loading for house size is positive (if that loading is effectively zero, use a documented deterministic fallback). These are reporting conventions only: signs are mathematically arbitrary.         "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run standardized PCA across 39 century observations and save analysis\", \"command\": \"cat > /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators over 100-year BCE observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\nDATA = Path(__file__).parent / 'data' / 'capital.csv'\\nOUT = Path(__file__).parent / 'pca_capital_diagnostics.txt'\\nSYMBOLIC = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\nFEATURES = ['ZAxtSchwert', 'ZDolch', 'Zhausgr', 'ZCU_AU', 'Zamber', 'ZMonument',\\n            'ZHort', 'ZSichel', 'ZBeil', 'ZMW', 'ZKeform', 'Zkeverz']\\n\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, dtype=str)\\n# Preserve period labels, parse European decimal-comma feature values explicitly.\\ndf['BCE'] = pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nXdf = df[FEATURES].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\\nassert Xdf.shape == (39, 12) and not Xdf.isna().any().any()\\nassert df['BCE'].nunique() == 39 and np.all(np.diff(df['BCE'].to_numpy()) == -100)\\nZ = StandardScaler().fit_transform(Xdf)\\npca = PCA(n_components=2, svd_solver='full')\\nscores = pca.fit_transform(Z)\\n# sklearn components_ rows are unit loading vectors; flip signs by prespecified conventions.\\nloadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\nif loadings[ [FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC], 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1; scores[:, 0] *= -1\\npc2_house = loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1]\\npc2_rule = 'house-size loading > 0'\\nif abs(pc2_house) < 1e-12:\\n    # Deterministic fallback: orient the PC2 loading with largest magnitude positive.\\n    j = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:, 1])))\\n    if loadings[j, 1] < 0:\\n        loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n    pc2_rule = f'house-size loading near zero; largest-absolute loading ({FEATURES[j]}) > 0'\\nelif pc2_house < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1; scores[:, 1] *= -1\\n\\n# Compute interpretable PC2 temporal score distribution and rank extremes.\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df['BCE'], 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\nsummary = score_df['PC2_score'].describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9])\\norder = score_df.sort_values('PC2_score')\\n# PC score scaling is sklearn's conventional score (projection of standardized rows).\\nlines = []\\nlines.append('PCA capital indicators: 39 century observations x 12 features; decimal commas parsed.')\\nlines.append('Features: ' + ', '.join(FEATURES))\\nlines.append('BCE coverage: %d to %d; step=100 years; rows=%d; missing feature cells=%d' %\\n             (df.BCE.min(), df.BCE.max(), len(df), int(Xdf.isna().sum().sum())))\\nlines.append('Preprocessing: centered/unit-variance StandardScaler; all 12 features; excluded BCE/group/color.')\\nlines.append('Variance explained PC1=%.6f (%.2f%%), PC2=%.6f (%.2f%%), cumulative=%.6f (%.2f%%)' %\\n             (pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[0],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[1],pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum(),100*pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum()))\\nlines.append('PC1 sign convention: sum of three symbolic feature loadings positive (sum=%.9f).' % loadings[[FEATURES.index(v) for v in SYMBOLIC],0].sum())\\nlines.append('PC2 sign convention: '+pc2_rule+' (house-size PC2 loading=%.9f).' % loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'),1])\\nlines.append('Feature loadings (unit component-vector coefficients), named symbolic features:')\\nfor v, label in [('Zhausgr','House size'),('ZDolch','Daggers'),('ZAxtSchwert','Hatchets/swords')]:\\n    i=FEATURES.index(v); lines.append('%s (%s): PC1=%+.6f, PC2=%+.6f' % (label,v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('All feature loadings PC1 / PC2:')\\nfor i,v in enumerate(FEATURES): lines.append('%s %+.6f %+.6f' % (v,loadings[i,0],loadings[i,1]))\\nlines.append('PC2 century-score distribution (39 scores):')\\nfor k in ['mean','std','min','10%','25%','50%','75%','90%','max']:\\n    lines.append('%s=%+.6f' % (k,summary[k]))\\nlines.append('Lowest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.head(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('Highest five PC2 scores (BCE, score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.4f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in order.tail(5).itertuples()))\\nlines.append('All century PC2 scores (BCE:score): '+', '.join('%d:%+.3f'%(r.BCE,r.PC2_score) for r in score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).itertuples()))\\nOUT.write_text('\\\\n'.join(lines)+'\\\\n')\\nprint(OUT.read_text())\\nPY\\npython /workspace/pca_capital_analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Here 'hundred-year slices' are the 39 temporal observations in one PCA, not separate PCAs per slice. With only one row per century, fitting an independent PCA within each century is not estimable. A rolling/multi-century PCA or a PCA using only the three named features would be a different analysis and is not what these reported estimates represent. The staged repository has no original PCA script, so this is a reproducible operational analysis rather than confirmation of an unavailable original workflow.", "evidence": "Executed analysis reports 39\u00d712 complete feature matrix, BCE range 500\u20134300 at 100-year spacing, zero missing feature cells. PC1/PC2 explained variance is 30.33%/19.47%. Named-feature loadings: Zhausgr +0.225080/+0.327440; ZDolch +0.265566/+0.074203; ZAxtSchwert +0.298602/+0.053953 (PC1/PC2). Century PC2 score summary: mean ~0, SD 1.528356, min \u22123.647895 (4300, 4200, 4100 BCE), median +0.339954, max +2.502965 (3200 BCE). The executable script and full diagnostics are saved in workspace; script compiled successfully after execution.", "assumptions": ["Primary operational interpretation is one PCA with 39 century rows as observations and 12 indicators as features.", "Feature columns were sample-standardized (correlation-matrix PCA); unit-variance standardization is an explicit analytic choice, not established from absent original code.", "PC1/PC2 signs were oriented using the agreed conventions; PCA signs themselves are arbitrary."], "uncertainties": ["No original PCA source workflow is staged, so the exact historical analysis method is unknown.", "Alternative meanings of PC2 distribution or hundred-year-slice PCA could produce different summaries; results here specifically distinguish feature loadings and century observation scores.", "The PCA is descriptive and does not by itself imply causal or substantive symbolic-capital interpretations."], "payload": {"hypothesis": "In a standardized PCA of all 12 capital indicators across the 39 century observations, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords all load positively on PC1 (+0.225, +0.266, +0.299, respectively). The century observations themselves are broadly distributed along PC2 (mean ~0, SD 1.53, range \u22123.65 to +2.50), with the most negative scores in 4300\u20134100 BCE and the largest positive score at 3200 BCE. Sign conventions are arbitrary; this answer orients PC1 so the three symbolic loadings sum positive and PC2 so house size is positive.", "workflow": "Executed pca_capital_analysis.py on data/capital.csv.
